## Words 3,001 – 3,100

### 3001. [abolish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abolish) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbɒlɪʃ/` · **US:** `/əˈbɑːlɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abo/aboli/abolish__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abo/aboli/abolish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This tax should be abolished.

### 3002. [abrupt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abrupt)
- [Oxford Learner's Dictionary Search](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abrupt)

### 3003. [absurd](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/absurd) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əbˈsɜːd/` · **US:** `/əbˈsɜːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abs/absur/absurd__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abs/absur/absurd__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That uniform makes the guards look absurd.
  - Of course it's not true, what an absurd idea.

### 3124. [concede](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/concede) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːd/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcede__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcede__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + speech ‘Not bad,’ she conceded grudgingly.
  - concede (that)… He was forced to concede (that) there might be difficulties.
  - concede something I had to concede the logic of this.
  - concede something to somebody He reluctantly conceded the point to me.

### 3125. [conceal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conceal) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːl/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - conceal somebody/something The paintings were concealed beneath a thick layer of plaster.
  - Tim could barely conceal his disappointment.
  - She sat down to conceal the fact that she was trembling.
  - conceal somebody/something from somebody/something For a long time his death was concealed from her.

### 3126. [conceive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conceive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːv/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He conceived the idea of transforming the old power station into an arts centre.
  - The dam project was originally conceived in 1977.

- **Examples:**
  - Dreams can be a rich source of inspiration for an artist.
  - Both poets drew their inspiration from the countryside.

### 3498. [inspire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inspire) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈspaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈspaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inspi/inspire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inspi/inspire__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - inspire somebody The actors' enthusiasm inspired the kids.
  - inspire somebody with something The actors inspired the kids with their enthusiasm.
  - inspire somebody to something His superb play inspired the team to a thrilling 5–0 win.
  - inspire somebody to do something By visiting schools, the actors hope to inspire children to put on their own productions.

### 3702. [notify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notif/notify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notif/notify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - notify somebody Competition winners will be notified by post.
  - You must notify us in writing if you wish to cancel your subscription.
  - notify somebody of something The police must be notified of the date of the demonstration.
  - notify something to somebody The date of the demonstration must be notified to the police.

- **Examples:**
  - a notorious criminal

- **UK:** `/ˌrekəɡˈnɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌrekəɡˈnɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recog/recognition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recog/recognition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He glanced briefly towards her but there was no sign of recognition.
  - the automatic recognition of handwriting and printed text by computer

### 3991. [recognize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recognize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrekəɡnaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈrekəɡnaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recog/recognize__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recog/recognize__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - recognize somebody/something I recognized him as soon as he came in the room.
  - Do you recognize this tune?
  - You might not recognize the name but you'll know her face.
  - I recognized the voice immediately.
